# SB-P-1.17 — MC-375A G10-07 Source Evidence Package

**Mission:** SB-P-1.17 — Manager Operations\
**Stage:** Source 18 v1.2 Stage 5 — Gate 10 open\
**Tracking:** MC373A-G10-07-01\
**Mode:** READ-ONLY evidence production. No repository file, branch, commit or PR was created or changed\
**Locked revision:** `main@ed62d81b480c8461b96e4d86b579030c3a955582`\
**Prepared by:** Claude Code, Definition Actor (the same actor that wrote the Stage 2 inventory and FCTM; see G.4)\
**Implementation Authorization:** WITHHELD — IMPLEMENTATION NOT AUTHORIZED.

MC-373A and MC-374A are not repository records. Their findings were not available to me as text, so this package works directly from the canonical sources named in MC-375A and does not restate MC-345, MC-348, MC-373A or MC-374A conclusions.

## Contents

- A. Canonical revision register
- B. 118-unit source ledger, plus units beyond the 118
- C. 116-row Part D reconciliation
- D. Upstream owner and acceptance ledger
- E. Grouping and splitting count bridge
- F. Discrepancy and unresolved-evidence register
- G. Validation totals and completeness attestation
- H. Obligation-level coverage of the 22 non-primary contracts, and the excluded-unit ledger

**Lock status of upstream specifications (checked, not assumed).** The Blueprint files of SB-P-1.15 and SB-P-1.16 still carry their pre-lock status lines inside the file. The governing records are the Stage 8 lock records above: SB-P-1.15 blob `0ab2ad6f` locked by MC-81 (record 82), SB-P-1.16 blob `305571a6` approved and locked through record 10 (confirmed by record 13). Both blobs equal the current files. SB-P-1.12 to SB-P-1.16 all record Implementation Authorization **WITHHELD**, so **no runtime acceptance exists for any upstream obligation**.

**Method (reproducible).** The Stage 2 parser (`## N.` sections; `b`/`n`/`p` locators; lead-in = prose ending in `:`) and keyword pattern (inventory §1) were re-run on the blobs above. They reproduce inventory §4 exactly: 371 hits, every per-contract count equal.

## B. 118-unit source ledger

**How the 118 were identified.** Inventory §4 publishes counts only. I reconstructed the set two ways:

1. **Independently:** I resolved every Part D pointer to its parser unit (61 rows by locator; 55 upstream-style pointers by reading the source).
2. **From the Stage 2 working list** kept outside the repository (not canonical).

Method 1 carries all 118 working-list units and 19 more (B.2). Per-contract counts of the working list equal the inventory column exactly. 117 of the 118 are keyword hits; the 118th, C24 §18 n15, is not (D-05).

**Column key.** *Unit:* review ID `<contract>-§<section>-<locator>`, then the Stage 2 working ID. *Meaning* is taken from the mapped row kind; "conditional" marks source wording such as "where", "when" or "if". *Verification* uses the MC-375A vocabulary.

## D. Upstream owner and acceptance ledger

All 116 Part D rows are listed. 103 are owned outside SB-P-1.17; the other 13 are owned here under an upstream assignment. *Upstream acceptance* cites the owning mission's locked Blueprint traceability and feasibility lines (line numbers in the blob in A) with their verification class and status. ### D.1 The two named identifier-granularity differences

- **`3-§3-7` versus upstream `3-§3`.** Local `3-§3-7` is C3 §3 b7 ("respectful coexistence with existing notebooks, POS and spreadsheets.").
  - SB-P-1.14 carries the whole of C3 §3 as one principle row `3-§3`, IN SCOPE SB-P-1.14 ("seven listed principles; single rule; realized by 3-§6-*, 3-§8-*, 3-§16-*, 3-§20-*"). SB-P-1.16 carries it as section row `3-§3`, DELEGATED to SB-P-1.14.
  - Owner and disposition agree (SB-P-1.14, DELEGATED here). Upstream acceptance is Blueprint SB-P-1.14 L1715: §8.1, V-DES, PASS (documentary).
  - The local row is a finer-grained identifier of one unit inside the upstream single rule, so the grouping is justified.
  - One convention point remains. FCTM §0.9 says delegated rows use the upstream identifier, and the upstream identifier here is `3-§3`. `3-§3-7` collides with no upstream row, but it is not an upstream ID (register D-11, documentation only).
- **`22-§4` versus upstream `22-§4-1`…`-4`.** Local `22-§4` carries C22 §4 b3 (suppliers) and b4 (inventory/catalog). It inherits the section-level ID from SB-P-1.12 (`22-§4` ASSIGNED SB-P-1.14) and SB-P-1.15 (`22-§4` DELEGATED SB-P-1.14).
  - SB-P-1.14 rows the section as `22-§4-1` (one model), `22-§4-2` (linked domain facts such as those listed, illustrative), `22-§4-3` (feature owns its domain model) and `22-§4-4` (no second truth). Both units belong to `22-§4-2`.
  - The grouping is justified, and owner and disposition agree. The evidence cell cites `I-14 (22-§4-4)`, which is the wrong upstream row (D-03).
  - Upstream identifiers are also inconsistent across missions: SB-P-1.13 uses `22-§4-2` for "no second truth" (D-09, informational).

## E. Grouping and splitting count bridge

**Why 118 and 116 differ.** The 118 count source units, at the granularity of the Stage 2 parser. The 116 count FCTM rows, at the granularity of the upstream owners' identifiers (FCTM §0.9). Three operations move between them:

- **Grouping:** several units go into one upstream row (−).
- **Splitting:** one unit is carried by two upstream rows (+).
- **Adding:** a row whose unit is not among the 118 (+).

Per contract: `rows = judged − grouping + splitting + added`.

| Contract | Inventory hits | Regenerated hits | Inventory judged | 118 set | of which hits | Grouping (−) | Splitting (+) | Added rows (+) | Added row IDs | Computed rows | Part D rows | Inventory rows | Check |

| C2 | 61 | 61 | 17 | 17 | 17 | 0 | 0 | 0 | — | 17 | 17 | 17 | OK |
| C3 | 23 | 23 | 9 | 9 | 9 | 0 | 1 | 1 | `3-§11-6` | 11 | 11 | 11 | OK |
| C4 | 29 | 29 | 11 | 11 | 11 | 0 | 0 | 0 | — | 11 | 11 | 11 | OK |
| C5 | 11 | 11 | 7 | 7 | 7 | 0 | 1 | 0 | — | 8 | 8 | 8 | OK |

| C14 | 3 | 3 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | — | 0 | 0 | 0 | OK |
| C15 | 5 | 5 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 0 | 0 | 0 | — | 3 | 3 | 3 | OK |

| C19 | 2 | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | — | 0 | 0 | 0 | OK |
| C20 | 13 | 13 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 1 | 0 | 0 | — | 2 | 2 | 2 | OK |
| C21 | 25 | 25 | 7 | 7 | 7 | 2 | 0 | 8 | `21-§4-2`, `21-§11-2`, `21-§12-1`, `21-§12-2`, `21-§12-3`, `21-§12-4`, `21-§12-5`, `21-§24-3` | 13 | 13 | 13 | OK |

| **Total** | **371** | **371** | **118** | **118** | **117** | **13** | **2** | **9** | | **116** | **116** | **116** | |

**Bridge in one line:** 118 units − 13 grouped + 2 split + 9 added = 116 rows.

- **Grouped (−13):**
  - C12 −2: the three §9 vocabulary items go into one row.
  - C20 −1: §5 b6 and b8 go into one section row.
  - C21 −2: §13 p1, b1 and p3 go into one row.
  - C22 −1: §4 b3 and b4 go into one row.
  - C24 −7: §6 2→1, §9 3→1, §18 5→1.
- **Split (+2):** C3 §9 b2 → `3-§9-2` and `3-§9-3`; C5 §4 b9 → `5-§4-9` and `5-§4-10`.
- **Added (+9):**
  - C3: `3-§11-6`, by joint upstream assignment (its unit is C3 §11 p4, not a keyword hit).
  - C21: `21-§4-2`, `21-§11-2`, `21-§12-1`…`-5` and `21-§24-3`.

C21 therefore moves 7 → 7 − 2 + 8 = 13.

The rows above give the exact per-contract arithmetic. Inventory §4 is reproduced exactly for every contract except one presentation point: `21-§24-3` is counted as added although its unit is a keyword hit (D-04).

### G.2 Integrity controls (MC-375A §6)

1. **Every one of the 118 has a ledger disposition:** yes (B, 118 rows).
2. **Every one of the 116 Part D rows is accounted for:** yes (C, 116 rows); three carry discrepancies (D-01 to D-03).
3. **Every applicable upstream-owned obligation has its ownership and acceptance relationship checked:** yes for ownership. For acceptance, 19 later-mission or decision-open rows are UNRESOLVED by nature (U-01, U-03), and runtime acceptance is absent everywhere (U-02).
4. **Units beyond keyword matches considered:** yes, by two supplementary screens and two structural checks (H.1). Residual method limitation U-04.
5. **No gaps hidden by aggregation:** every grouped or section-level row is decomposed to units (B, B.2, C, E); every excluded hit is listed individually (H.2).
6. **Discrepancies and gaps explicit:** F.
7. **Reproducible:** every count and table is generated from the blobs in A by the parser and patterns stated in H.1. The judgement inputs are explicit (Part D unit map in C; per-unit classes in H).

### G.3 Attestation

**Ledger coverage is complete.** B covers all 118 units, C all 116 rows, D all 116 upstream relationships, and H all 4,899 units of the 22 non-primary contracts.

**Acceptance evidence is not complete,** and this package does not claim it is:
- 19 rows have no owning-mission artefact yet (U-01, U-03).
- No upstream obligation has runtime acceptance (U-02).
- Three FCTM identifier or citation errors (D-01 to D-03) and one consistency decision (D-08) are open for Mission Control.

Nothing in this package changes the FCTM, inventory, Blueprint or any source, and nothing here passes Gate 10.

### G.4 Independence limitation

The actor that wrote the Stage 2 inventory and FCTM also produced this package. The parser, patterns and unit map are mine. The upstream FCTMs and Blueprints are the only cross-authored evidence. Independent Engineering Review Room 2 should treat B to E as reproducible claims to re-run, not as independent confirmation.

## H. Obligation-level coverage of the 22 non-primary contracts

### H.1 Screens and classes

**Screens:**
- **(1) Keyword screen:** inventory §1 pattern.
- **(2) Supplementary domain-term screen**, applied to all 22 contracts: `catalog|product master|item mapping|batch|threshold|low-stock|shift|till|cash drawer|cash|variance|Reference Cost|margin|standing rule|delegated automation|bounded automation|procurement|vendor|price list|unit of measure|packag|SKU|barcode`.
- **(3) C21 authority screen:** `delegat|grant|Employee|role|revok|revoc|scope|external participant|Owner-only|financial intelligence|analytics|cost|margin|export|audit`.
- **(4) Upstream inheritance:** every upstream FCTM row naming SB-P-1.17 or a C7/C13/C17 identifier.
- **(5) Dependency references:** every other-contract foundation referenced by C7/C13/C17 maps to an FCTM dependency code.
